Cut-offdate: 22MAY2025

Open-label Treatment Period (After Week 16 and up to Week 52)

A total of 93/215 subjects (43.3%) had at least 1 major protocol deviation during the open label treatment period (TSIDV01Ob). The most common major protocol deviation classifications (i.e. reported in ≥20 subjects overall) were visits and assessments (53 subjects, 24.7%), (non /) investigational medicinal product (34 subjects, 15.8%), and ICF (21 subjects, 9.8%).

Discrepancies in Study Allocation, Stratification, and Risk Factor Status

During data verification, before database lock, discrepancies were identified in Study A and Study B allocation and between the stratification factors and risk factor status recorded in the CRFs and those used in the randomization dataset (RTSM).

The randomisation was conducted using the stratification factors captured in RTSM. Based on review and sensitivity analyses performed for the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints, the discrepancies in study allocation and stratification were confirmed not to have a material impact on study population, randomization balance, or on the interpretation of efficacy outcomes.

Data reconciliation process and the number of affected subjects were documented in a Blinded Data Review Meeting Summary and are summarised below:

Study Allocation

In total, 11 (4.6%) subjects were allocated to Study B (nr-axSpA), who should have been assigned to Study A (r-axSpA): 8 subjects in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 3 in the placebo group (TSIDM02b).

Discrepancies in Stratification

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- CRP strata: 3 subjects had discrepancies between RTSM (used for stratification) and CRF; stratified to the ≤5 mg/L group but had &gt;5 mg/L per CRF : 1 subject in each treatment group, and stratified to the &gt;5 mg/L group but had ≤5 mg/L per CRF: 1 subject in the filgotinib 200 mg group.
- bDMARD strata: 5 subjects were mis stratified (all in the placebo group). Of these, 2 subjects were stratified as IR (per RTSM) but should have been stratified as naïve/non-IR, and 3 subjects were stratified as naïve/non-IR but should have been stratified as IR.

## Discrepancies in Risk Factor Status

Per CRF, 48/237 (20.3%) subjects were aged ≥65 years and/or had pre-specified risk factors. Of these 48 subjects, 42 were identified as such at the start of the study (per RTSM) and were intended to be exposed to the lowest effective dose during the open label treatment period. Of the remaining 6 subjects, 2 had risk factors per the data collected but were incorrectly enrolled as non-risk subjects, and 4 were enrolled under the initial protocol, which did not differentiate by risk factor status.

## Intercurrent Events

At least 1 intercurrent event (i.e. the use of any rescue medication and/or IP discontinuation) was reported for 12 subjects. A total of 7 subjects were considered to have stopped IP before their Week 16 ASAS40 assessment. This total includes the 5 subjects who discontinued IP during the double-blind treatment period, plus 2 additional subjects who completed double blind exposure but whose last IP intake occurred at least 1 day before the Week 16 ASAS40 assessment; for these 2 subjects, the Week 16 ASAS40 was therefore considered post IP discontinuation.

A total of 6 subjects reported use of rescue medication before their Week 16 ASAS40 assessment and during the double-blind treatment period. In addition, 23 subjects had major protocol deviations classified as intercurrent event, of which 17 subjects with ASAS40 response at Week 16 did not report prior IP discontinuation or use of rescue medication. An additional 2 subjects reported a significant interruption of IP. All subjects with intercurrent events were included in the analyses in accordance with the pre-specified ASAS40 estimand framework: permanent discontinuation of IP and use of rescue medication were handled using either a composite or treatment policy strategy depending on the estimand, whereas other protocol deviations were always handled under a treatment policy strategy. Given the similar distribution across both groups, the applicant states that this protocol deviations are unlikely to introduce substantial differential bias in the treatment comparison.

All sensitivity analyses defined for the primary main estimand (i.e. using a MAR approach for handling missing data, repeating the main analysis by considering the actual stratification, and repeating the main analysis by considering the actual stratification and the actual study classification) were consistent with the results of the main analysis of the corresponding estimand. For all combinations tested in the tipping point analysis by considering missing outcomes as responders in a stepwise manner (i.e. up to 2 missing values for placebo and up to 1 missing value for filgotinib), the study conclusion did not reverse for the primary main estimand.

## Primary Supplementary Estimand 1

When using all data collected after IP discontinuation and/or use of any rescue medication in the statistical analysis, the proportion of responders increased in the filgotinib 200 mg group only (4 additional filgotinib 200 mg subjects were responders compared to the main estimand analysis). The ASAS40 response rate at Week 16 was statistically significantly higher in the filgotinib 200 mg group, compared with the placebo group (42.6% vs 20.9%; treatment difference of 21.7%, 95% CI 10.7 to 32.8, p value &lt;0.001).

## Primary Supplementary Estimand 2

When using all data collected after use of any rescue medication in the statistical analysis but considering a subject as non-responder as soon as they discontinued IP, the proportion of responders increased in the filgotinib 200 mg group only (1 additional filgotinib 200 mg subject was a responder compared to the main estimand analysis). The ASAS40 response rate at Week 16 was statistically significantly higher in the filgotinib 200 mg group, compared with the placebo group (40.3% vs 20.9%; treatment difference of 19.4%, 95% CI 8.4 to 30.4, p value &lt;0.001)

Overall, 84.6% of the subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg with an ASAS40 response at Week 16 maintained response at Week 52. Among the subjects initially on placebo without ASAS40 response at Week 16, 65.3% achieved ASAS40 response by Week 52 after switching to filgotinib in the open label treatment period. ## Intercurrent Events

At least 1 intercurrent event (i.e. the use of any rescue medication and/or IP discontinuation) was reported for 24 subjects.

A total of 21 subjects were considered to have stopped IP before their Week 16 ASAS40 assessment. This total includes the 17 subjects who discontinued IP during the double-blind treatment period, plus 4 additional subjects who completed double blind exposure, but whose last IP intake occurred at least 1 day before the Week 16 ASAS40 assessment; for these 4 subjects, the Week 16 ASAS40 was therefore considered post IP discontinuation.

A total of 9 subjects reported use of rescue medication before their Week 16 ASAS40 assessment, while 10 subjects reported use of rescue medication during the double-blind treatment period. One subject reported use of rescue medication during the double-blind treatment period but after their Week 16 ASAS40 assessment; for this subject, the Week 16 ASAS40 was therefore not considered post rescue medication.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In addition, 23 subjects had major protocol deviations classified as intercurrent events, of which 13 subjects with ASAS40 response at Week 16 did not report prior IP discontinuation or use of rescue medication. No additional subjects reported a significant interruption of IP. All subjects with intercurrent events were included in the analyses in accordance with the pre-specified ASAS40 estimand framework: permanent discontinuation of IP and use of rescue medication were handled using either a composite or treatment policy strategy depending on the estimand, whereas other protocol deviations were always handled under a treatment policy strategy.

## Sensitivity Analyses

All sensitivity analyses defined for the primary main estimand (i.e. using a MAR approach for handling missing data, repeating the main analysis by considering the actual stratification, and repeating the main analysis by considering the actual stratification and the actual study classification) were consistent with the results of the main analysis of the corresponding estimand.

For all combinations tested in the tipping point analysis by considering missing outcomes as responders in a stepwise manner (i.e. up to 3 missing values for placebo and up to 2 missing values for filgotinib), the study conclusion did not reverse for the primary main estimand.

## Primary Supplementary Estimand 1

When using all data collected after IP discontinuation and/or use of any rescue medication in the statistical analysis, the proportion of responders increased in the placebo group only (4 additional placebo subjects were responders compared to the main estimand analysis). The ASAS40 response rate at Week 16 was statistically significantly higher in the filgotinib 200 mg group, compared with the placebo group (34.5% vs 21.2%; treatment difference of 13.3%, 95% CI 2.0 to 24.5, p value=0.022).

All sensitivity analyses defined for the primary supplementary estimand 1 (i.e. using a MAR or a MNAR approach for handling missing data) were consistent with the results of the main analysis of this estimand.

## Primary Supplementary Estimand 2

The primary supplementary estimand 2 considered subjects as non-responder as soon as they discontinued IP, but used all data collected after use of any rescue medication not leading to IP discontinuation in the statistical analysis. The treatment effect observed for the primary supplementary estimand 2 was similar to this from the primary main estimand: of the 3 subjects in the placebo group who used rescue medication only before Week 16, none was a responder at Week 16.

## Secondary Efficacy Endpoints

Change from Baseline in ASDAS CRP at Week 16

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Change from Baseline in SPARCC MRI score of SIJs at Week 16

At Baseline, SPARCC MRI Score of SIJ mean (SD) was 5.28 (8.806) in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 5.61 (9.139) in the placebo group.

<!-- image -->

LS means were derived from an ANCOVA model including treatment, baseline value, and stratification factors (screening hs-

CRP and prior use of bDMARDs) as fixed effects.

Handling of Intercurrent Events: All data collected after intercurrent events (IP discontinuation/Use of any Rescue Medication) were used in the statistical analysis (Treatment Policy).

Missing Data Handling: Missing-at-Random (MAR).

Baseline was defined as the last non-missing value before the first IP administration.

Cut-off date: 22MAY2025

Figure 20. SPARCC MRI Score of SIJs Least-Squares Mean CFB: Secondary Main Estimand (Study B)

Due to the high proportion of low or zero values observed at baseline and for changes from baseline at Week 16 during the blinded data review, a prospectively defined exploratory nonparametric comparison of the empirical cumulative distribution functions has been performed on changes from baseline at Week 16. This non parametric test confirmed a difference in the distribution of the data between treatment groups with a higher proportion of subjects on filgotinib showing improvement (43.2% in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 28.8% in the placebo group; data on file) and a lower proportion of subjects on filgotinib showing worsening (11.7% in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 32.7% in the placebo group; data on file) compared with placebo.

## Additional endpoints and week 52 data

## ASAS40 Through Week 52

<!-- image -->

Handling of Intercurrent Events: Subjects considered as non-responder as soon as they discontinued P (Composite [NRI]) but all data collected after use of any rescue medication not leading to IP discontinuation were used in the statistical analysis (Treatment Policy).

Missing Data Handling: Non-Responder Imputation (NRI).

Cut-off date: 22MAY2025

Figure 24. ASAS40 Response up to Week 52: Main Estimand (Study B)

Overall, 78.0% of the subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg with an ASAS40 response at Week 16 maintained response at Week 52. Among the subjects initially on placebo without ASAS40 response at Week 16, 60.2% achieved ASAS40 response by Week 52 after switching to filgotinib in the open label treatment period. In addition, 55.7% of the subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg without ASAS40 response at Week 16 achieved a response by Week 52.

## hs-CRP

The hs-CRP levels improved by Week 4 in the filgotinib 200 mg group and were sustained through Week 16, but remained stable through Week 16 in the placebo group. For subjects who continued on filgotinib after Week 16, the hs-CRP levels remained stable through Week 52.

Handling of intercurrent events:

- DB+OL main estimand (CSR): subjects considered as non-responder as soon as they discontinue IP (Composite [NRI]) but all data collected after use of any rescue medication not leading to IP discontinuation were used in the statistical analysis (Treatment Policy)
- Supplementary estimand - new analysis: Subjects are considered non-responders as soon as they discontinue study treatment and/or initiate rescue medication (composite strategy, NRI), irrespective of whether the event occurs during the DB period (up to Week 16) or during the OL period.

In case multiple intercurrent events occur, the first occurring event is used to define the reason for imputation.

Missing Data Handling: Non-responder imputation (NRI).

## Handling of Intercurrent Events:

- composite: patients considered as non-responder as soon as they discontinue treatment and/or use any rescue medication after Week 16.
- treatment policy: patients considered as non-responder as soon as they discontinued IP, but all data

## Results and Analysis

## Primary analysis (Primary and secondary endpoints at Week 16)

Categorical endpoints were analyzed using a Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel test, stratified by the stratification factors; a composite strategy was applied (patients discontinuing treatment or receiving rescue medication were considered non-responders), with missing data handled using non-responder imputation (NRI). Continuous endpoints were analyzed using MMRM, including baseline, treatment, visit, treatment-by-visit interaction, and stratification factors; SPARCC MRI SIJ was analyzed using ANCOVA. A treatment-policy strategy was applied.

## Supportive study

The Phase 2 study TORTUGA is a completed randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter proof of concept study to assess the efficacy and safety of filgotinib 200 mg administered once daily (q.d.) for 12 weeks in subjects with active AS.

Subjects were randomised in a 1:1 ratio to receive filgotinib 200 mg q.d. or placebo q.d for a duration of 12 weeks. Randomisation to treatment groups was stratified by current csDMARD use (yes or no) and prior tumour necrosis factor (TNF) inhibitor use (yes or no).

Figure 29. TORTUGA: Schematic Diagram of Study Design

<!-- image -->

The primary endpoint was to evaluate the effect of filgotinib 200 mg q.d. compared with placebo q.d. on the signs and symptoms of AS, as assessed by the change from baseline (CFB) in ASDAS at Week 12.

## Selected secondary endpoints:

- Signs and symptoms of AS: Assessment of SpondyloArthitis international Society (ASAS) core set and Bath Ankylosing Spondylitis Disease Activity Index (BASDAI) at Week 12.
- Physical function: Bath Ankylosing Spondylitis Functional Index (BASFI) at Week 12.
- Spinal mobility: Bath Ankylosing Spondylitis Metrology Index (BASMI) at Week 12.
- Spinal and sacroiliac joint (SIJ) inflammation: Spondyloarthritis Research Consortium of Canada (SPARCC) magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) at Week 12.
- Enthesitis: Maastricht Ankylosing Spondylitis Enthesitis Score (MASES) at Week 12.
- Fatigue and General Quality of life: Functional Assessment of Chronic Illness Therapy (FACIT)- Fatigue, 36-item short-form (SF-36) health survey, and Ankylosing Spondylitis Quality of Life (ASQoL) questionnaire at Week 12.

Eligible subjects were male or female subjects ≥18 years of age with active AS with documented evidence of fulfilling the Modified New York criteria for AS at screening and had radiographic sacroiliitis confirmed by central reading; had active AS with BASDAI ≥4 and spinal pain ≥4 at screening and baseline; had serum hs-CRP ≥0.3 mg/dL at screening; and had documented inadequate response to 2 or more NSAIDs, including cyclooxygenase 2 inhibitors at the therapeutic dose range for a total duration of at least 4 weeks.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A total of 263 subjects were screened, of whom 116 subjects were randomised and treated q.d. with either filgotinib 200 mg (58 subjects) or placebo (58 subjects). Nine subjects discontinued the study due to safety reasons (1 subject in the filgotinib 200 mg group), efficacy reasons (1 subject in the placebo group), or other reasons (i.e. lost to FU [4 subjects, of whom 2 in each treatment group], consent withdrawal [2 subjects in the placebo group], or protocol deviation [1 subject in the placebo group]). The study was completed by 107 subjects. Of all subjects in the main study, 99 subjects also enrolled in the optional PK substudy and 9 subjects enrolled in the optional PD substudy Demographic and other baseline characteristics (including disease characteristics, prior medication for AS, and prior and concurrent medical conditions or procedures) were reasonably balanced between the treatment groups, except for the mean baseline SPARCC MRI score of spine, which was higher in the filgotinib 200 mg group compared with the placebo group (19.0 vs 13.8). All subjects were white and from non-Hispanic or non-Latino ethnicity, with a mean (standard deviation [SD]) age of 42 (10.3) years. The majority of subjects (74.1%) were males. The mean (SD) duration of AS since diagnosis was 7 (6.7) years. The majority of subjects (81.9%) had enthesitis, and about two thirds (64.7%) had hs-CRP ≥upper limit of normal (ULN; 10 mg/L). The baseline disease characteristics were as expected for the study population with active AS, with a mean (SD) ASDAS score of 4.2 (0.71) and a mean (SD) BASDAI score of 7.0 (1.24). About one third of subjects (38.8%) used csDMARDs at baseline, while prior use of a TNF inhibitor was reported in a minority of subjects (9.5%). Stratification by concurrent csDMARD use and prior TNF

inhibitor use was adequate between both treatment groups.

## Efficacy Results

The primary endpoint in this study was the CFB in the ASDAS score, used to assess AS disease activity in terms of signs and symptoms, at Week 12. In the primary analysis, the ASDAS CFB at Week 12 in the filgotinib 200 mg group was compared with the placebo group by means of a superiority test at a 5% 2-sided significance level. The null hypothesis, stating that the ASDAS CFB in the filgotinib 200 mg group was equal to the ASDAS CFB in the placebo group, was rejected, supporting the primary objective of this study. Between-group differences were statistically significant (p &lt;0.0001) from Week 1 through Week 12 (mean CFB of -1.5 in the filgotinib 200 mg group vs -0.6 in the placebo group [p &lt;0.0001, ANCOVA] at Week 12, with last observation carried forward [LOCF] imputation). Sensitivity analyses of the primary endpoint (i.e. on the observed cases [OC] population, the Per-Protocol Analysis Set, or using a mixed model for repeated measures [MMRM] approach) confirmed the results of the primary analysis. Note that all secondary endpoints were exploratory in nature; analyses were descriptive and not confirmatory.

## 2.4.3. Discussion on clinical efficacy

With this submission, the MAH initially applied to add the following new indication for Jyseleca (filgotinib):

Axial spondyloarthritis

## Non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis (nr-axSpA)

Jyseleca is indicated for the treatment of active non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis in adult patients with objective signs of inflammation as indicated by elevated C-reactive protein (CRP) and/or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), who have responded inadequately or are intolerant to nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs).

## Ankylosing spondylitis (AS, radiographic axial spondyloarthritis [r-axSpA])

Jyseleca is indicated for the treatment of active ankylosing spondylitis (radiographic axial spondyloarthritis) in adult patients who have responded inadequately or are intolerant to conventional therapy.

## Design and conduct of clinical studies

This application was supported by the data from two currently ongoing phase 3 multicenter studies (OLONGUITO, study A and study B), conducted under a single protocol. The first part of the study was a 16-week randomised, double-blind, parallel-group, placebo-controlled period where patients with raxSpA (Study A) and nr-axSpA (study B) were randomised 1:1 to filgotinib 200 mg once daily (QD) or placebo. The selection of the 200 mg dose was based on the efficacy and safety results of the completed 12-week phase 2 study (TORTUGA) which only evaluated filgotinib at the 200 mg dose versus placebo (116 patients included).

In both studies A and B, patients were stratified by baseline hsCRP and prior use of bDMARD.

The double-blind period was followed by an open label treatment period, where all patients &lt;65 years of age and without risk factors for MACE, VTE or malignancy were treated Jyseleca 200 mg up to week 52.

Patients above 65 years of age and/or with the specific risk factors for events mentioned above were treated with the lowest effective dose (100 mg or 200 mg) based on their initial response up to week 104. The investigation of the lowest effective dose in patients with risk factors was made in consideration of the outcome of the Article 20 procedure on JAK inhibitors used for chronic inflammatory conditions (EMEA/H-A20/1517/C/005113/0014). After week 52, patients &lt;65 years or age and without the specific risk factors who achieved a sustained ASDAS-CRP&lt; 2.1 at weeks 40 and 52 were re-randomised (1:1) to either 200 mg or 100 mg filgotinib up to week 104. The investigation of a lower dose is encouraged by the CHMP and in line with the applicable Guideline (EMA/CPMP/EWP/4891/03 Rev.1, Corr 1*). This part of the study investigating dose de-escalation was ongoing during the procedure. The final results of this part of the study, which is classified as category 3 in the RMP, are expected to be submitted for review in Q2 2027.

The design of studies A and B was in general in line with the Guideline on the clinical Investigation of Medical products for the treatment of Axial Spondyloarthritis (EMA/CPMP/EWP/4891/03 Rev.1, Corr 1*), and with the CHMP scientific advice given on 23 June 2022 (EMA/SA/0000086316).

At the cut-off date, all subjects had either discontinued prematurely or completed 52 weeks of the study.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Subjects in the study were to be adults (≥ 18 years of age) and have a clinical diagnosis of axSpA (radiographic or non-radiographic) meeting the ASAS criteria. The subjects were to have an active disease defined by having a BASDAI score ≥ 4 and a Spinal pain score ≥ 4 based on a 0 to 10 NRS. In addition, they had to have an inadequate response to at least 2 NSAIDs over a period of at least 4week in total at the maximum recommended doses or had an intolerance to at least 2 NSAIDs. Previous use of no more than 2 bDMARDs was permitted. Concomitant medication with csDMARD was permitted if stable (methotrexate, leflunomide, sulfasalazine or hydroxychloroquine). The inclusion and exclusion criteria were adequate and reflecting the intended population in the proposed indication (SmPC Section 4.1). Eligible patients were randomised in a ratio 1:1 to either filgotinib 200 mg once daily or placebo for the first 16 weeks. After week 16, all patients were to have filgotinib open label. During the placebocontrolled phase, rescue medication was not allowed to be initiated or adjusted and if used, the patient was considered a non-responder in the primary analysis. Rescue therapy was however allowed after week 16 and included adding or modifying doses of NSAID, analgesics, corticosteroids, csDMARD. The primary endpoint ASAS40 is considered the preferred ASAS response criterion in the applicable Guideline (EMA/CPMP/EWP/4891/03 Rev.1, Corr 1*), hence, the choice of the endpoint was supported. The study had, in addition, 5 defined multiplicity-controlled secondary endpoints (ASDAS CRP, SPARCC MRI score of SIJs, BASFI, ASQoL, BASMI). It was noted that ASDAS-CRP change from baseline was used. However, the EMA guideline (EMA/CPMP/EWP/4891/03 Rev.1, Corr 1*) states that \"in order to facilitate interpretation of the clinical relevance of the observed effect, responder analyses are preferable over mean absolute changes, but both should be provided\". Although not included as a multiplicity-controlled endpoint, responder analyses were also conducted. As more effective therapies become available for axSpA, disease remission or low disease activity is increasingly regarded as an appropriate therapeutic goal. Thus, the CHMP acknowledged the provided analysis regarding partial remission or low disease activity (i.e. ASDAS inactive disease (ID) &lt;1.3, ASDAS low disease activity &lt;2.1. In addition, the MAH included BASMI as an evaluation of spinal mobility, BASFI as an evaluation of physical function, ASQoL as a measurement of quality of life and SPARCC-score as a measurement of structural changes (MRI-changes). The primary and secondary endpoints used in this study enabled evaluation of inflammatory, symptomatic, structural and functional aspects of AxSpA. The study was conducted between 5 April 2023 (first subject first visit) and 22 May 2025 (Week 52 last subject last visit) in 91 sites in 18 countries. A total of 915 patients were screened and 495 patients were randomised (258 in study A and 237 in study B). In study A (r-axSpA), 129 patients were randomised to filgotinib 200 mg and 129 to placebo. During the first period (the 16-week placebo-controlled period), 4 patients in the filgotinib group (3%) and 1 patient in the placebo group (1%) discontinued the study, the main reason was due to AEs. A total of 251 patients (97%), continued to the OL phase (123 patients from the filgotinib group and 128 patients from the placebo group) and &gt;90% in both groups completed week 52 on treatment. In study B (nr-axSpA), 119 patients were randomised to filgotinib 200 mg and 118 to placebo. During the first period (the 16-week placebo-controlled period), 5 patients in the filgotinib group (4%) and 12 patients in the placebo group (10%) discontinued the study, the main reason due to AEs in the filgotinib group, and lack of efficacy, lost to follow-up and withdrawal of consent in the placebo group. A total of 215 patients, (91%) continued to the OL phase (111 patients from the filgotinib group and

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

104 patients from the placebo group) and 86% in both the filgotinib group and 80.5% in the placebo group completed week 52 on treatment. Also, in both studies A and B, the subgroup of patients &gt;65 years of age and/or with specific risk factors the proportion of patients completing the study on treatment was rather high (84-96% depending on group). The protocol was amended 3 times during the clinical study. The main amendments pertained to a dose-reduction in patients &gt;65 years of age and/or with pre-specified risk factors, update of study estimand and sponsorship transfer. The protocol amendments were not considered to impact on the study integrity. A total of 31% of patients in study A and 33% of patients in study B had at least 1 major protocol deviation during the placebo-controlled phase. The most common deviation involved \"(non /) investigational medicinal product\" and were due to lack of returns of empty bottles. In addition, there were some discrepancies in study allocation (e.g. r-axSpA vs nr-axSpA), stratifications factors and risk factor status. However, this was adequately addressed and was not considered to have any major impact on the efficacy results. Baseline demographics and disease characteristics were mainly comparable between the treatment and placebo groups within the two studies. In the r-axSpA population (study A), the majority of the included patients were male (77.5%), white (86.4%) and European (84.5%). The mean age was 42.8 years; the mean duration of symptoms was 15.8 years and the mean duration since diagnosis of 8.7 years. Around 36% of the patients were bDMARD experienced, 82.6% used NSAID at baseline and 24.8% used csDMARDs. In the nr-axSpA population, the frequency of females and males were similar. However as in the raxSpA population, the majority of the patients were white (92.8%) and from Europe (89.5%). Mean age was 40.9 years, mean duration of symptoms (11.6 years) and duration since diagnosis (5.3 years), thus slightly lower than the r-axSpA population. Around 31% of the patients were bDMARD experienced, 80.6% used NSAID and 21.5% used csDMARD at baseline. With regard to hs-CRP at baseline (mg/L) [CRF], mean (SD), the value was 10.445 (16.7891) for Study A and 9.378 (16.5421) for study B. No information regarding baseline spinal pain was provided. The initially proposed indication stated that it was intended for the treatment of patients with r-axSpA who have responded inadequately or are intolerant to conventional therapy and for patients with nraxSpA who have responded inadequately or are intolerant to nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs). The wording of the proposed indication was questioned by the CHMP due to the lack of information on whether patients with intolerance to NSAID/ conventional therapy were included in the study. The MAH acknowledged that the clinical dataset is not representative of a population primarily defined by intolerance, and that generalisation of efficacy results to patients intolerant to NSAIDs was not fully supported. Accordingly, the MAH revised the indication wording by removing reference to intolerance. This was endorsed by the CHMP. The numbers of patients &gt; 65 years of age and/or with specific risk factors consisted of 72/258 (27.9%) of the population in study A and 48/237 (20.3%) in study B. The risk factors that affected most of the patients were current or past smoking. Only 11 patients were &gt;65 years of age. Estimands and Statistical methods The analysis methods used in this study were CMH test for binary endpoints and MMRM and ANCOVA for continuous endpoints. Those are standard and acceptable to the CHMP. All efficacy analyses were performed on the FAS which in this study included all randomised subjects.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

For the primary responder endpoint, a composite estimand was used where data after IP discontinuation or use of rescue medications were disregarded and subjects were considered nonresponders. The composite approach for the ICE of rescue medication was accepted, while the composite approach for the ICE of IP discontinuation was not the most preferred for regulatory purposes. However, since a supplementary treatment policy estimand was also presented, the approach was found acceptable. For the secondary continuous endpoints, a treatment policy strategy was used for all ICEs. This was accepted since the number of subjects using rescue medications was larger in the placebo group and hence, the method was considered conservative for the treatment comparison. A supplementary estimand with a while on treatment approach was also presented but was considered of less importance. The primary method for handling missing data in binary endpoints was non-responder imputation (NRI). This method is not conservative for treatment comparisons if the amount of missing data is larger in the placebo arm. However, sensitivity analyses using MI were performed showing robustness of results in both studies (A and B). For continuous endpoints, MAR assumptions were used as primary analysis method. MI based on MNAR assumptions were used in sensitivity analysis for both binary and continuous endpoints with similar results as primary analyses. The MI with MNAR assumptions used a copy increment from reference approach. A Jump to Reference approach would have been preferable, however, since the amount of missing data and ICEs were small in both studies (particularly in Study A), results were considered sufficiently robust and non-dependent of statistical methods. Multiplicity was controlled within in Studies A and B at the 5% two-sided significance level. Hierarchical testing and the Bonferroni-Holm procedure was used, which was acceptable to the CHMP. For the analysis on open-label data up to week 52, use of rescue medications was handled with a treatment policy strategy. The supplementary estimand where data after use of rescue medication was disregarded and subjects considered as non-responders was considered more useful for regulatory purposes. No week 52 endpoints were included in the multiple testing procedure.

## Efficacy data and additional analyses

In r-axSpA patients (study A), a statistically significant higher proportion of patients in the filgotinib group reached ASAS40 at week 16 in comparison to the placebo group (39.5% vs 20.9%; treatment difference of 18.6%, 95% CI 7.6 to 29.6, p=0.001). The result was supported by all planned sensitivity analysis, a tipping point analysis and when using supplementary estimands. A significant difference between the filgotinib treated r-axSpA patients and the placebo group were apparent in four (ASDAS CRP, SPARCC MRI score of SIJs, BASFI and ASQoL) of the five multiplicitycontrolled endpoints. There was no significant difference in BASMI (i.e. spinal mobility), in both groups a small improvement in mobility was seen, although it could hardly be considered clinically relevant. In the nr-axSpA population (study B), a statistically significant higher proportion of patients in the filgotinib group reached ASAS40 at week 16 in comparison to the placebo group (34.5% vs 17.8%; treatment difference of 16.7%; 95% CI 5.7 to 27.6, p=0.003). Only two (ASDAS CRP, SPARCC MRI score of SIJs) of the five secondary endpoints were significantly different from the placebo group. In addition, the proportion of patients with intercurrent events and protocol deviations were more pronounced in this group, especially in the placebo group. However, the result on the primary endpoint seen was supported by all planned sensitivity analyses, a tipping point analysis and when using supplementary estimand. Considering that the third secondary endpoint (mean change from baseline of BASFI at Week 16) did not reach a statistically significance, ASQoL and BASMI were considered as exploratory only, and were therefore interpreted with caution.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A numerical difference in ASAS40 responses was observed with filgotinib 200 mg treatment over placebo as from week 1 and at all subsequent timepoints up to week 16 in Study A and B. The results regarding ASAS40 seemed consistent in the majority of analysed subgroups such as age, gender, disease duration, disease activity and prior use of bDMARD. However, for patients with concomitant csDMARD, the point estimate was close to zero in both the r-axSpA population and nraxSpA populations and for some subgroups, there were discrepancies between the findings in r-axSpA and nr axSpA group. However, based on the small numbers in these subgroups, no firm conclusion could be made. After week 16, all patients still in the study received filgotinib OL. The percentage of subjects achieving an ASAS40 response continued to increase through Week 52 in both subjects who continued on filgotinib after Week 16 and in subjects who switched from placebo. In study A, 84.6% of subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg with an ASAS40 response at Week 16 maintained this response at Week 52 and of the subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg without an ASAS40 response at Week 16, 54.9% achieved a response by Week 52. In study B, a similar result was seen with 78.0% of subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg with an ASAS40 response at Week 16 maintained this response at Week 52 and of the subjects initially on filgotinib 200 mg without an ASAS40 response at Week 16, 55.7% achieved a response by Week 52. However, these numbers were based on OL treatment without a control group and adjustment of concomitant treatment for axSpA was allowed during this period, including NSAID, corticosteroids, pain medications and csDMARD. Upon the CHMP's request, the MAH clarified that only a few patients (3 of 140 patients (2.1%)) received rescue medication after Week 16 during the OL phase and provided additional analyses indicating that efficacy was maintained through 52 weeks for the majority of patients who had a response at Week 16. Thus, the CHMP endorsed the inclusion of a statement on maintenance of efficacy up to Week 52 in SmPC Section 5.1. The available data support the proposed posology of 200 mg once daily in adult patients. Upon the CHMP's request, the MAH provided additional data showing that some patients with initial partial response may respond after the initial 16 weeks. Hence, the CHMP agreed to recommend consideration of treatment discontinuation in patients who have shown no clinical response after 16 weeks and that the decision to continue treatment should be based on individual clinical assessment, as some patients with initial partial response may subsequently improve with continued treatment beyond Week 16. Regarding the elderlies, there were only 13 patients above 65 years of age (whereof 2 patients ≥ 75 years of age) in the two studies (i.e. 4/258 in study A and 9/237 in study B). One (1) of 2 patients randomised to filgotinib in study A and 2/6 patients in study B achieved an ASAS40 response at week 16. None of the patients randomised to placebo (i.e. 5 patients) achieved an ASAS40 response at week 16. However, when switching to active treatment also the majority of the patients on initial placebo seemed to be able to achieve a ASAS40 response at some point during week 16 to week 52. At week 52, 8/13 patients had an ASAS40 response, including some patients that had switched to 100 mg at some timepoint during the study. Acknowledging the limited number of subjects, these results supported demonstration of efficacy of filgotinib in patients with axSpA above 65 years of age. Posology in patients aged over 65 years or with risk factors for VTE, MACE and malignancy In studies A and B, patients aged over 65 years or with risk factors who had achieved a response at 16 weeks received the lowest effective dose (100 mg or 200 mg) during the OL phase. The investigation of the lowest effective dose in these patients at risk was made in consideration of the outcome of the Article 20 procedure on JAK inhibitors used for chronic inflammatory conditions (EMEA/HA20/1517/C/005113/0014). Upon request from the CHMP, the MAH provided efficacy analyses at Week 52 for the patients with risk factors who received an ASAS40 response at week 16 (i.e. 11 patients in the r-axSpA study and 8 patients in the nr-axSpA study). All these 19 patients received a reduced dose

to 100 mg and around 33% maintained a response up until week 52 on the reduced dose. However, the majority of patients needed a dose escalation (10 patients) and of these, 6 patients were able to regain an ASAS40 response after the increased dose. In some patients, the dose was also reduced once again when a response was received with maintained disease control. Acknowledging the limited number of subjects, these results were considered sufficient to support a dose reduction to 100 mg in patients who achieved a response at week 16 and at increased risk of VTE, MACE and malignancy and in patients aged 65 years of age and older (see also Discussion on clinical safety). Treatment should be discontinued in patients who have shown no clinical response after 16 weeks of treatment. Further, in case of disease flare, the dose may be increased to 200 mg. For long term treatment, the lowest effective dose should be used.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.4.4. Conclusions on the clinical efficacy

A clinically relevant and robust short-term effect as measured by ASAS40 has been demonstrated for filgotinib 200 mg in the target population of subjects with active axSpA, both r-axSpA and nr-axSpA. Although less prominent in the nr-axSpA population, these results were supported by the secondary endpoints measuring different aspects of the disease such as disease activity, physical function, health related outcome and spinal mobility. Maintenance of efficacy of filgotinib was shown through 52 weeks for the majority of patients who had a response at Week 16. The agreed therapeutic indication in axial spondyloarthritis is for the treatment of:

- active non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis in adult patients with objective signs of inflammation as indicated by elevated C-reactive protein (CRP) and/or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), who have responded inadequately to nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs).
- active ankylosing spondylitis (radiographic axial spondyloarthritis) in adult patients who have responded inadequately to conventional therapy.

## 2.5. Clinical safety

For the purpose of this document, the following definitions apply: 'Adverse event - AE' means any untoward medical occurrence in a subject to whom a medicinal product is administered, and which does not necessarily have a causal relationship with this treatment. 'Serious adverse event - SAE' means any untoward medical occurrence that at any dose requires inpatient hospitalisation or prolongation of existing hospitalisation, results in persistent or significant disability or incapacity, results in a congenital anomaly or birth defect, is life-threatening, or results in death. The definition (in line with ICH E2A) includes important medical events that may not be immediately life-threatening or result in death or hospitalisation but may jeopardise the patient or may require intervention to prevent one of the other outcomes listed in the definition above. 'Adverse Drug Reaction - ADR' means any untoward and unintended response to a medicinal product related to any dose administered, for which, after thorough assessment, a causal relationship between the medicinal product and the adverse event is at least a reasonable possibility, based for example, on their comparative incidence in clinical trials, or on findings from epidemiological studies and/or on an evaluation of causality from individual case reports.

## Adverse events

Unless specified otherwise, all presented AEs and laboratory data were treatment-emergent (TE). Treatment-emergent AEs (TEAEs) were defined as having a start date equal to or after the date of the first administration of the study drug and no later than 30 days after the last administration of the study drug. Treatment-emergent laboratory abnormalities were defined as values that increase by at least 1 toxicity grade from baseline, observed at a postbaseline time point no later than 30 days after last administration of study drug, and collected under fasted conditions for lipids and glucose assessments.

## Common TEAEs

## DB pool

The most common SOCs for both treatment groups were (≥5% of subjects in any treatment group):

- Infections and infestations
- o Filgotinib 200 mg group: EAIR = 68.90/100 PYE (95% CI: 52.32; 89.07)
- o Placebo group: EAIR = 84.65/100 PYE (95% CI: 65.74; 107.32)
- o EAIR difference = -15.76/100 PYE (95% CI: -43.84; 11.90)
- Gastrointestinal disorders
- o Filgotinib 200 mg group: EAIR = 25.62/100 PYE (95% CI: 16.24; 38.45)
- o Placebo group: EAIR = 17.94/100 PYE (95% CI: 10.26; 29.14)
- o EAIR difference = 7.68/100 PYE (95% CI: -6.93; 22.63)
- Metabolism and nutrition disorders
- o Filgotinib 200 mg group: EAIR = 25.35/100 PYE (95% CI: 16.07; 38.04)
- o Placebo group: EAIR = 6.58/100 PYE (95% CI: 65.74; 2.42; 14.32)
- o EAIR difference = 18.77/100 PYE (95% CI: 6.68; 32.12)
- Investigations
- o Filgotinib 200 mg group: EAIR = 23.06/100 PYE (95% CI: 14.27; 35.25)
- o Placebo group: EAIR = 16.72/100 PYE (95% CI: 9.36; 27.58)
- o EAIR difference = 6.34/100 PYE (95% CI: -7.63; 20.58)
- Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders
- o Filgotinib 200 mg group: EAIR = 20.68/100 PYE (95% CI: 12.45; 32.29)
- o Placebo group: EAIR = 24.78/100 PYE (95% CI: 15.53; 37.52)
- o EAIR difference = -4.10/100 PYE (95% CI: -19.27; 10.74)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

According to the MAH, the EAIRs of common TEAEs were comparable across both treatment groups, apart from a higher EAIR in the filgotinib 200 mg group compared with the placebo group for hyperlipidaemia (7.50 E/100 PY versus 0 E/100 PY) and hypercholesterolemia (10.72 E/100 PY versus 3.27 E/100 PY).

## Serious adverse event/deaths/other significant events

## Deaths

One death was reported in the overall pool and occurred during the open-label treatment period of the OLINGUITO study. On Day 257, a patient without protocol-pre-specified risk factors, who had switched from placebo to filgotinib on day 112, died due to an acute myocardial infarction after a septic embolus (caused by a bronchopneumonia), resulting in a cardiogenic shock. The event was considered not related to study drug by the investigator. In the investigator's opinion, alternative causality included a pre-existing condition (i.e. axSpA) and intercurrent illness (i.e. infections: bronchopneumonia and necrotised colitis). The MAH put forward that it is known that infections may trigger acute cardiovascular events and that systemic or localised infections significantly increase the risk of thrombosis and are independent risk factors for thromboembolic diseases such as cardiovascular disease (myocardial infarction) and others. In addition, the MAH argued that autoimmune diseases such as axSpA are responsible for a prothrombotic status and increase the risk of cardiovascular disease including myocardial infarction.

## Serious AEs

In the DB pool, the EAIRs for SAEs were 5.32 E/100 PY in the filgotinib 200 mg group versus 4.34 E/100 PY in the placebo group (EAIR difference = 0.98 E/100 PY [95% CI: -6.69; 8.75]).

In the overall data, the EAIRs for SAEs were 4.40 E/100 PY in the FIL Total group, 3.95 E/100 PY in the FIL200 group, and 5.95 E/100 PY in the LED Total group. Apart from pneumonia (reported as SAE under 3 different PTs), all other SAEs by PT occurred in no more than 1 subject.

## Adverse events of special interest (AEIs)

AEIs include positively adjudicated MACE and thromboembolic events (VTE and ASTE), infections, malignancies (excluding NMSC), NMSC, gastrointestinal (GI) perforations, fractures, and hyperlipidaemia.

## Laboratory findings

The following laboratory abnormalities of interest were selected: liver-related abnormalities and those associated with the established adverse drug reaction profile of filgotinib (based on RA and UC studies), including neutropenia, lymphopenia, blood creatine phosphokinase increased, blood phosphorus decreased, and hypercholesterolemia.

EAIR = exposure-adjusted incidence rate per 100 cPYE; cPYE = censored PYE (PYE until onset first event if subject has an event, else total PYE). The 95% CI of the EAIR is the exact 95% CI estimated based on the Poisson distribution (Ulm 1990). The 95% CI for the EAIR difference is estimated using the method of variance estimates recovery (Li et al. 2011).

Neutrophils results are not assessed in TORTUGA. Instead, segmented neutrophils were assessed which are not included in the analysis. For LDL Cholesterol, in OLINGUITO, LDL results correspond to all results from calculated or direct LDL whereas in TORTUGA, only calculated LDL was recorded.

The data cut-off date for the ongoing OLINGUITO study is 22MAY2025, or the date of the first dose of study drug in the DDE period for subjects who were re-randomized into the DDE period.

Subjects with at least one post-baseline assessment are included per laboratory test. The overall laboratory category result includes subjects with data

from at least one post-baseline laboratory test in that category.

The EAIRs for phosphate decrease of Grade 1 or higher were numerically higher in the filgotinib 200 mg group compared with the placebo group:

- Filgotinib 200 mg group: EAIR = 46.32/100 PYE (95% CI: 33.24; 62.84)
- Placebo group: EAIR = 18.84/100 PYE (95% CI: 10.97; 30.16)

There were no phosphate decreases of Grade 3 or higher or hypophosphatemia TEAEs reported in the DB Pool

In the overall pool, the most common laboratory abnormalities of Grade 3 or higher (≥1% of subjects in the FIL Total group) were creatine kinase increase (EAIR of 4.01 E/100 PY), AST increase (EAIR of 1.54 E/100 PY), and fasting LDL cholesterol increase (EAIR of 6.80 E/100 PY).

In the overall pool, Grade 3 or higher ALT and AST elevations occurred in 0.6% (3/498) and 0.8% (4/498) of patients receiving filgotinib 200 mg, respectively.

Figures of interest of laboratory parameters over time from the pivotal studies OLINGUITO A and B are shown in this AR.

## Race

A majority of subjects were white (91.8% in the DB pool). As the numbers of Black or African subjects were low (N=1) and the numbers of subjects with other race were also low (N = 11 in the DB Pool), no meaningful comparisons with these subgroups could be made according to the applicant.

## Risk factors

This risk factor subgroup analysis was only applied for the OLINGUITO study. In OLINGUITO, 120 (24.2%) subjects were aged ≥65 years and/or had other pre-specified risk factors. The pre-specified risk factors in addition to being aged ≥65 years were: history of atherosclerotic cardiovascular (CV) disease (i.e. myocardial infarction, [un]stable angina pectoris or coronary artery procedures), previous VTE, prior malignancy including non-melanoma skin cancer (NMSC), or other major risk factors for these events (such as current or past smoking [&gt;10 pack-years]). The main risk factors were current smoking (13.5%), past smoking (3.8%), and age ≥65 years (2.2%).

## Fertility, pregnancy and lactation

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

In the clinical studies of filgotinib, male and female subjects of childbearing potential who engage in heterosexual intercourse had to have agreed to use protocol-specified methods of contraception. During TORTUGA study, no subject pregnancies were reported. During OLINGUITO study, one subject in Study A who was randomised to placebo in the double-blind treatment period was reported with pregnancy during the open-label treatment period. The subject was discontinued due to pregnancy. The gestational age, the estimated date of delivery, and the number of foetuses were not reported. Further pregnancy information could not be provided, as the

subject decided not to sign the optional Pregnancy ICF during the last visit.

## Safety related to drug-drug interactions and other interactions

According to the applicant, no new findings relevant to the coadministration of filgotinib with other drugs were available.

## Post marketing experience

The Applicant did not provide any new postmarketing data with this application but refers to that postmarketing data of filgotinib are reviewed within periodic safety update report (PSUR) procedures.

## 2.5.1. Discussion on clinical safety

In the current application, the safety data for filgotinib in patients with active axSpA were based on two studies; TORTUGA (completed) and OLINGUITO (cut-off date 22 May 2025). The two studies had similar populations and treatment regimens and were therefore analysed together based on two pooling strategies: DB pool (12-week [TORTUGA] or 16-week [OLINGUITO], double-blind study periods) and Overall pool (double-blind and open-label study periods up to 52 weeks).

Exposure A total of 538 subjects with axSpA have received at least 1 dose of filgotinib for a total of 459.2 PY across the TORTUGA and OLINGUITO studies. In the placebo-controlled periods, 306 subjects received at least one dose of filgotinib 200 mg with a mean individual exposure of 15.1 weeks, and 305 subjects were treated with placebo with a mean individual exposure of 14.8 weeks. The extent of exposure was deemed sufficient to support the proposed extension of indication. Adverse events During the 12-16-week placebo-controlled periods, the overall rate of AEs was comparable across filgotinib 200 mg and placebo groups. Exposure-adjusted incidence rates for AEs considered by the investigator to be related to treatment tended to be higher in the filgotinib group (41.97 E/100 PY [95% CI 29.55-57.86]) than in the placebo group (24.90 E/100 PY [15.60-37.69]). The EAIRs of serious AEs and adverse events leading to discontinuation were comparable across filgotinib and placebo groups. No deaths were reported in the DB Pool. In the overall data (filgotinib doses of 200 mg q.d. or lowest effective dose [LED] from studies TORTUGA and OLINGUITO including both placebo-controlled and open-label treatment periods for up to 52 weeks), the EAIRs of TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to treatment was higher in the 'filgotinib 200 mg q.d. group' (22.75 E/100 PY) than in the 'LED total' group (10.43 E/100 PY). There was one death in the filgotinib 200 mg group, which is discussed below. In the 12-16-week placebo-controlled period, the most common TEAE by PT (≥4% of subjects in any

treatment group) was nasopharyngitis. This was more commonly reported in the placebo group than in the filgotinib group (22.32 and 16.35 E/100 PY, respectively). Overall, the EAIRs of common TEAEs were comparable across both treatment groups, apart from a higher EAIR in the filgotinib 200 mg group compared with the placebo group for hyperlipidaemia (7.50 E/100 PY versus 0 E/100 PY) and hypercholesterolemia (10.72 E/100 PY versus 3.27 E/100 PY). Hypercholesterolemia is listed as an uncommon adverse reaction in SmPC section 4.8 and monitoring guidance for hyperlipidaemia is given in SmPC section 4.2. Hence, no product information update was deemed necessary. In the overall data, the most common TEAEs (≥4% of subjects in the FIL Total group) were nasopharyngitis, upper respiratory tract infection, hypertension, and blood creatine phosphokinase increased. Adverse events related to study drug In the 12-16 week placebo controlled periods, the most common TEAEs related to study drug by PT (≥1% of subjects in any treatment group) were blood creatine phosphokinase increased, hypercholesterolemia, nausea, and upper respiratory tract infection. Drug-related TEAEs were more commonly reported in the filgotinib 200 mg group (12.1%) than in the placebo group (7.2%). This was primarily driven by a higher incidence of increased blood creatine phosphokinase (5 cases, 1.6%) and hypercholesterolaemia (5 cases, 1.6%), which are both known adverse events of filgotinib and listed as adverse drug reactions in the product information. In the overall data, the most common TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to the study drug were blood creatine phosphokinase increased, myositis, and hypercholesterolemia, all of which were reported only in the filgotinib 200 mg group. Notably, myositis was reported in 7 patients with axSpA treated with filgotinib in the Overall pool (EAIR: 1.54 E/100 PY). One case occurred during the placebo-controlled period, and 6 cases during the uncontrolled phase. However, all cases of myositis were reported from a single site, were mild in severity (Grade 1), not associated with any concomitant muscle weakness or myalgia, and not clinically confirmed. According to the MAH, all myositis events coincided with elevations in creatine kinase (CK), and represent asymptomatic, or mildly symptomatic, CK elevations rather than clinically confirmed cases of myositis. CK elevations are reflected adequately in the SmPC. No further action was deemed necessary. Deaths There was one death due to acute myocardial infarction due to septic embolus claimed by the MAH to be secondary to bronchopneumonia. This occurred in a patient without known risk factors for cardiovascular disease at inclusion. Fatal cardiovascular events have been reported also in the studies of filgotinib in RA and UC and the risk is further discussed below. Overall, one death in the studies of filgotinib in axSpA corresponds to 0.2 E/100 PY, which is in line with the EAIRs for TEAEs leading to death reported in the studies of filgotinib in other indications (RA and UC). Although the event was considered not related to the study drug by the investigator, this fatal event was regarded as potentially related to treatment in the overall assessment of benefit/risk considering the identified risk for serious and opportunistic infections, and the potential risk for MACE associated with filgotinib. This case is further discussed under Adverse events of special interest. Serious AEs No treatment-emergent serious adverse event occurred in more than one subject except for pneumonia (PTs: Pneumonia, Pneumonia bacterial, and Pneumonia staphylococcal), which occurred in three subjects receiving filgotinib 200 mg in the placebo-controlled period. These events are further discussed under Adverse events of special interest. No events of pneumonia occurred in the placebo group.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Adverse events of special interest (AEIs) AEIs included MACE and thromboembolic events, infections, malignancies (excluding NMSC), NMSC, GI perforations, fractures, and hyperlipidaemia. There was a fatal MACE event in the filgotinib 200 mg group. No additional cases of MACE occurred in subjects treated with filgotinib. In addition, there was one potential case of venous thromboembolism in the placebo-controlled TORTUGA study. MACE and VTE are listed as important potential risks in the RMP. Warning related to these risks are implemented in the product information. Hence, no further action was deemed necessary. In the placebo-controlled period, the incidence rate of infections was similar in the filgotinib 200 mg and placebo groups (68.90 [95% CI: 52.32-89.07] and 81.83 [95% CI: 63.29-104.11] E/100 PY, respectively). Serious infections occurred in 3 subjects in the filgotinib group (3.19 E/100 PY) and not at all in the placebo group. Overall, in subjects treated with filgotinib, 7 subjects were reported with a serious infection (1.53 E/100 PY), 3 subjects with herpes zoster (0.66 E/100 PY, all reported as nonserious and Grade 1 or 2 in severity), and 1 subject with an opportunistic infection (Grade 2 ophthalmic herpes simplex, 0.22 E/100 PY). The reported EAIRs were of the same magnitude as in the RA and UC populations. The use of filogitnib is contraindicated in patients with active tuberculosis or active serious infections and a warning on infections is already implemented in the product information. SmPC section 4.8 has been updated to describe the infections observed in patients with axSpA. Serious and opportunistic infections and Herpes zoster are listed in the RMP as important identified risks and will be further characterised in the Category 3 Study OLINGUITO GLPG0634-CL-336 (final results expected in Q2 2027). Warning related to these risks are already implemented in the product information. Notably, pneumonia occurred in three subjects in the filgotinib 200 mg group and was not observed in the placebo group. Pneumonia is listed as an adverse reaction (frequence uncommon) in the SmPC Section 4.8, and a warning on infections is included in SmPC section 4.4. Hence, no product information update was deemed necessary. There were two cases of malignancies in the placebo-controlled pool, both in the filgotinib 200 mg group (EAIR: 2.12 E/100 PY). In the overall data, malignancies were reported in 4 subjects treated with filgotinib 200 mg (EAIR: 0.87 E/100 PY), which is in line with what is expected for the population. There was no specific pattern observed with regards to malignancies. Malignancy and NMSC are important potential risks in the RMP; warnings related to these risks are already implemented in the product information. Hence, no further action was deemed necessary. No events of GI perforations were reported in the DB Pool. In total, 2 subjects in the overall data were reported with a GI perforation. One (1) subject in the FIL200 group was reported with diverticular perforation and 1 subject in the LED Total group with gastric perforation. These results did not evoke new concern. JAK inhibition has been linked to a potentially increased risk of bone fracture. Fractures is listed as an important potential risk in the RMP. In the studies of filgotinib in subjects with axSpA, there were no fractures reported in the placebo-controlled periods. In the overall data the EAIR for fractures was low (1.31 E/100 PY [n=6]). These results did not evoke new concern. Hyperlipidaemia was discussed above under Adverse events. Laboratory findings Haemoglobin levels increased compared with placebo during the 16-week placebo-controlled period (possibly reflecting response to therapy) and remained stable thereafter for up to 52 weeks, as

reflected in the SmPC section 5.1.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

A decrease in platelet counts within the first four weeks of filgotinib treatment that then remained stable was observed, which is in line with what was reported from studies of filgotinib in RA and UC. A similar initial decrease was observed in neutrophil counts. A risk for neutropenia is already included in the SmPC Sections 4.2, 4.4 and 4.8. No update of the product information was deemed necessary. A mean increase in liver transaminases (ALT and AST) was observed during the first 8 weeks of filgotinib 200 mg treatment compared with placebo, especially in the OLIGUINITO study A in r-AxSpA. The elevated levels remained relatively stable over time. No subjects had AST or ALT &gt;3 x ULN and concurrent total bilirubin &gt;2 x ULN or met Hy's Law in any treatment group. However, there was one case of drug-induced liver injury (grade 2) that led to permanent study drug discontinuation. This case was not accompanied by other markers of liver dysfunction and resolved with discontinuation of filgotinib and MTX. Sporadic cases of ALT or AST &gt;3x ULN occurred both with filgotinib and placebo, did not occur in combination with total bilirubin &gt;2xULN, and were not associated with any clinical sequelae. The CHMP considered that the observed early increases in transaminases are small in magnitude and plateau over time. Importantly, there were no reports of clinically significant hepatic injury associated with the transaminase elevations. Nevertheless, these elevations may be of clinical relevance, especially in light of the hepatotoxicity observed with other JAK inhibitors. Hence, the product information was updated to include ALT increased and AST increased as adverse reactions (frequency: uncommon). A description of the observed hepatic transaminase elevations was added in SmPC Section 4.8 as this is relevant information for the prescriber. An increase in mean plasma creatine kinase was observed in subjects who received filgotinib 200 mg. Grade 3 or higher creatine kinase increases occurred at an EAIR of 4.01 E/100 PY in the Overall pool and was slightly more common in the filgotinib group (1.6%, n=5) than in the placebo group 1.0%, n=3) during the placebo-controlled period. No cases of rhabdomyolysis were reported. Blood creatine phosphokinase increased is already listed as an adverse reaction (frequency uncommon) in SmPC Section 4.8 of the SmPC. No update was warranted. Consistent with findings in the RA studies, serum phosphate levels decreased in subjects treated with filgotinib compared with the placebo group. The EAIR for phosphate decrease of Grade 1 or higher was 46.32 E/100 PY in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 18.84 E/100 PY in the placebo group. No cases of Grade 3 or higher were reported. Blood phosphorus decreased is already listed as an adverse reaction (frequency common) in the SmPC section 4.8. No update was deemed necessary. Both HDL and LDL levels increased in subjects treated with filgotinib compared with placebo, resulting in increased total cholesterol. LDL/HDL ratios did not increase; there was rather a slight HDL/LDL increase observed. Hyperlipidemia is an important potential risk in the RMP. Laboratory changes in lipids changes are already addressed in SmPC Section 4.8 and a warning on lipids is also already implemented in SmPC Section 4.4. No further action was deemed necessary. Safety in special populations The risk for TEAEs was numerically slightly higher for females than for males, but the difference was not considered to be clinically meaningful, and a similar pattern was observed also for placebo. Due to the low numbers of patients in Black, African and other race subgroups, no conclusions could be made from the subgroup analysis on race. Patients aged over 65 years or with risk factors for VTE, MACE and malignancy In the OLINGUITIO study, subgroup analysis was performed based on a number of pre-specified risk factors (mainly age &gt;65 and smoker/past smoker). The incidence of TEAEs were comparable between

the filgotinib 200 mg group and the placebo group in both subgroups. The frequency of adverse events of interest such as MACE, VTE, serious infection, and malignancies did not occur at high enough frequency to provide meaningful subgroup analyses by risk factor status. Referring to findings in the RA studies with filgotinib, the MAH argued that the overall safety profile in patients aged ≥65 years and patients with risk factors was largely driven by baseline risk factors.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Only 14 subjects (9 in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 5 in the placebo group) aged ≥65 years were included in the placebo-controlled parts of the studies of filgotinib in patients with axSpA. Thus, no meaningful conclusions could be made on the comparison between filgotinib and placebo in this age group. No data was presented for patients with axSpA ≥75 years of age. No adjustment of the starting dose in elderly patients with axSpA was initially proposed by the MAH. Upon request from the CHMP, the MAH discussed findings in elderly in the RA studies and concluded that a higher EAIRs of several AEs were observed in patients aged ≥65 years compared with patients aged &lt;65 years across both 200 mg and 100 mg dose groups, although this was consistent with the expected increase in background risk with age. There was no clear dose-dependent increase in serious or life-threatening

AEs.

Thus, in order to minimise the safety risks known from the class of JAK inhibitors and data on filgotinib from other indications in patients 65 years of age and older and patients at increased risk for MACE, VTE and malignancy, the posology was adapted that the higher dose of 200 mg is recommended in these patients at risk with axSpA for short term treatment (16 weeks) followed by a reduction to 100 mg in case of a response (see also Discussion on clinical efficacy). Treatment should be discontinued in patients who have shown no clinical response after 16 weeks of treatment. Further, in case of disease flare, the dose may be escalated to 200 mg once daily. For long term treatment, the lowest effective dose should be used.

## Fertility, pregnancy and lactation

JAK inhibitors are known to be teratogenic, and filgotinib is contraindicated during pregnancy. One pregnancy was reported in the clinical studies of filgotinib in patients with axSpA.; no information was available on the outcome of this pregnancy.

No new information on the use of filgotinib during lactation or any fertility effects were included in this submission.

## 2.5.2. Conclusions on clinical safety

The most common adverse event in the placebo-controlled phase of the studies in AxSpA subjects was nasopharyngitis. The EAIRs of common TEAEs were comparable across both placebo and filgotinib groups, apart from a higher EAIR in the filgotinib 200 mg group compared with the placebo group for hyperlipidaemia and hypercholesterolemia. Hypercholesterolemia is an adverse reaction in the product information and monitoring guidance for hyperlipidaemia is given in SmPC section 4.2.

Regarding the important potential risks of MACE and VTE, there was a fatal MACE event in the filgotinib 200 mg group and one potential case of VTE reported in AxSpA subjects. Warning related to these risks are already implemented in the product information.

In the placebo-controlled period, the incidence rate of infections was similar in the filgotinib 200 mg and placebo groups. Serious infections occurred in 3 subjects, only in the filgotinib group. The reported EAIRs were of the same magnitude as in the RA and UC populations. This risk is addressed in the SmPC Sections 4.3, 4.4 and 4.8. Serious and opportunistic infections and Herpes zoster are important identified risks in the RMP and will be further characterised in the axSpA population through the completion of the Study OLINGUITO GLPG0634-CL-336 (Category 3 study in the RMP, final results expected in Q2 2027).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Malignancies were reported in 4 subjects treated with filgotinib 200 mg (EAIR: 0.87 E/100 PY), which is in line with what is expected for the population. Malignancy and NMSC are important potential risks in the RMP and warnings related to these risks are already implemented in the product information.

A mean increase in liver transaminases (ALT and AST) was observed during the first 8 weeks of filgotinib 200 mg treatment compared with placebo. Considering the hepatotoxicity observed with other JAK inhibitors and the potential clinical relevance of the reported elevations, ALT increased and AST increased were added as adverse reactions (frequency: uncommon) in the product information.

The safety profile of filgotinib in the axSpA indication was overall consistent with the observed safety profile in the RA and UC populations; the data provided supported the new indication in AxSpA.

## 2.5.3. PSUR cycle

The requirements for submission of periodic safety update reports for this medicinal product are set out in the list of Union reference dates (EURD list) provided for under Article 107c(7) of Directive 2001/83/EC and any subsequent updates published on the European medicines web-portal.
