## 2.7. Update of the Product information

As a consequence of this new indication, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 and 6.6 of the SmPC have been updated. The Package Leaflet has been updated accordingly.

## 2.7.1. User consultation

A justification for not performing a full user consultation with target patient groups on the package leaflet has been submitted by the MAH and has been found acceptable for the following reasons:

- the addition of the axial spondyloarthritis indication to the currently approved PIL has not introduced significant changes to the text or layout.
- no new additional information in safety leading to different instruction of use, the updated proposed Patient leaflet is not deemed to introduce significant changes to the text or layout other than the inclusion of this new indication in lay languages into the PIL.

## 3. Benefit-Risk Balance

## 3.1. Therapeutic Context

## 3.1.1. Disease or condition

AxSpA is a chronic inflammatory disease primarily affecting the SIJ and spine. AxSpA, includes both radiographic (capturing also the ankylosing spondylitis patient group) and non-radiographic forms.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

AxSpA typically manifests in individuals under the age of 45 years, with a peak onset in the third decade of life. Historically, it was considered a male dominated condition, but recent studies indicate a more balanced gender distribution, especially in nr-axSpA. The prevalence of axSpA varies globally, influenced by the distribution of the human leukocyte antigen B27 (HLA-B27) gene, which is strongly associated with the disease. In the early stages of the disease, active inflammation is the primary driver of symptoms, including pain, stiffness, fatigue, and poor quality of sleep. Over time, chronic inflammation can lead to structural changes, such as new bone formation, which contribute to loss of spinal mobility and impaired physical function. In both r-axSpA and nr-axSpA, many patients experience not only the core axial symptoms but also peripheral manifestations, including peripheral arthritis, enthesitis, and, to a lesser extent, dactylitis. In addition, extra-musculoskeletal manifestations, such as acute anterior uveitis, inflammatory bowel disease, and psoriasis are frequently observed and contribute to the overall disease burden and impaired quality of life.

The MAH initially applied for the following indication:

## Axial spondyloarthritis

## Non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis (nr-axSpA)

Jyseleca is indicated for the treatment of active non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis in adult patients with objective signs of inflammation as indicated by elevated C-reactive protein (CRP) and/or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), who have responded inadequately or are intolerant to nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs).

## Ankylosing spondylitis (AS, radiographic axial spondyloarthritis [r-axSpA])

Jyseleca is indicated for the treatment of active ankylosing spondylitis (radiographic axial spondyloarthritis) in adult patients who have responded inadequately or are intolerant to conventional therapy.

## 3.1.2. Available therapies and unmet medical need

Current treatment options for axSpA aim to reduce inflammation, manage pain, and maintain mobility. NSAIDs are typically the first line of treatment and are recommended by the joint ASAS- EULAR 2022 guidelines. Physical therapy and regular exercise are also crucial components of management, helping to preserve range of motion and improve overall function. For patients who do not respond adequately to NSAIDs, bDMARDs targeting TNF-alpha or IL 17 are commonly used. Other therapies, including JAK inhibitors, are also an option for this patient group. In severe cases, surgical interventions may be necessary to correct bone deformities or/and relieve pain. Despite available treatments, a substantial proportion of patients experience suboptimal responses to advanced therapies which can ultimately lead to irreversible structural damage, further loss of spinal mobility, functional limitations, and substantial impact on daily living and overall quality of life (Navarro-Compán et al. 2025).

## 3.1.3. Main clinical studies

This application was supported by data from two phase 3 multicenter studies (OLINGUITO, study A and B), conducted under a single protocol. Both studies were ongoing during the procedure. The first part of the study was a 16-week randomised, double-blind, parallel-group, placebo-controlled period where patients with r-axSpA (Study A) and nr-axSpA (study B) were randomised 1:1 to filgotinib 200 mg once daily (QD) or placebo. In both studies, patients were stratified by baseline hsCRP and prior use of bDMARD. The double-blind period was followed by an open label treatment period, where all patients &lt;65 years of age and without risk factors for MACE, VTE or malignancy were treated Jyseleca 200 mg up to week 52. Patients above 65 years of age and/or with the specific risk factors for events mentioned above were treated with the lowest effective dose (100 mg or 200 mg) based on their initial response from week 16 up to week 104.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

After week 52, patients &lt;65 years or age and without the specific risk factors who achieved a sustained ASDAS-CRP&lt; 2.1 at weeks 40 and 52 were rerandomised (1:1) to either 200 mg or 100 mg filgotinib up to week 104. The results of this part of the study, which is classified as category 3 in the RMP, are expected to be submitted for review in Q2 2027.

Subjects in the study were to be adults (≥ 18 years of age) and have a clinical diagnosis of axSpA (radiographic or non-radiographic) meeting the ASAS criteria. The subjects were to have an active disease defined by having a BASDAI score ≥ 4 and a Spinal pain score ≥ 4 based on a 0 to 10 NRS. In addition, they had to have an inadequate response to at least 2 NSAIDs over a period of at least 4week period in total at the maximum recommended doses or had an intolerance to at least 2 NSAIDs. Previous use of no more than 2 bDMARDs was permitted. Concomitant medication with csDMARD was permitted if stable (methotrexate, leflunomide, sulfasalazine or hydroxychloroquine). A total of 495 patients were randomised, 258 in study A and 237 in study B.

## 3.2. Favourable effects

In the r-axSpA ppopulation (study A), a statistically significant higher proportion of patients in the filgotinib group reached ASAS40 at week 16 in comparison to the placebo group (39.5% vs 20.9%; treatment difference of 18.6%, 95% CI 7.6 to 29.6, p=0.001). This result was supported by all planned sensitivity analyses, a tipping point analysis and when using supplementary estimand. A significant difference between the filgotinib group and the placebo group was reached for the multiplicity-controlled endpoints of ASDAS CRP, SPARCC MRI score of SIJs, BASFI and ASQoL.

In the nr-axSpA population (study B), a statistically significant higher proportion of patients in the filgotinib group reached ASAS40 at week 16 in comparison to the placebo group (34.5% vs 17.8%; treatment difference of 16.7%; 95% CI 5.7 to 27.6, p=0.003). This result was supported by all planned sensitivity analysis, a tipping point analysis and when using supplementary estimand. A significant difference between the filgotinib group and the placebo group was reached for the multiplicity-controlled endpoints of ASDAS CRP and SPARCC MRI score of SIJs.

The results regarding ASAS40 was consistent in the majority of analysed subgroups such as age, gender, disease duration, disease activity and prior use of bDMARD.

Maintenance of efficacy was studied in the OL phase where patients received filgotinib after week 16. The percentage of subjects achieving an ASAS40 response continued to increase through Week 52 in both subjects who continued on filgotinib after Week 16 and in subjects who switched from placebo. Acknowledging the limitations of the OL treatment without a control group and considering that only a few patients received rescue medication during the OL phase, the CHMP considered that the efficacy of filgotinib was shown through 52 weeks for the majority of patients who had a response at Week 16.

## 3.3. Uncertainties and limitations about favourable effects

The studied population was not representative of a population primarily defined by intolerance to NSAIDs for nr-axSpA or conventional therapy for r-axSpA. Therefore, upon request from the CHMP, the subgroups defined by intolerance were removed from the indication wording.

There were only 13 patients above 65 years of age in the two studies (i.e. 4/258 in study A and 9/237 in study B). One of 2 patients randomised to filgotinib in study A and 2/6 patients in study B achieved an ASAS40 response at week 16. None of the 5 patients randomised to placebo achieved an ACR 40

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

response at week 16. However, when switching to active treatment also the majority of the patients on initial placebo achieved an ACR 40 response between week 16 and week 52. At week 52, 8/13 patients had an ACR response. Acknowledging the limited number of subjects, these results supported demonstration of efficacy of filgotinib in patients with axSpA above 65 years of age. In subjects aged ≥65 years and/or with pre-specified risk factors, all patients received 200 mg in the placebo period. The dose was adjusted to the lowest effective dose (100 mg or 200 mg) during the OL phase due to safety reasons in consideration of the outcome of the Article 20 procedure on JAK inhibitors used for chronic inflammatory conditions (EMEA/H-A20/1517/C/005113/0014). Among the 19 patients with risk factors who received an ASAS40 response at week 16 (i.e. 11 patients in the raxSpA study and 8 patients in the nr-axSpA study), all received a reduced dose to 100 mg and around 33% maintained a response up until week 52 on the reduced dose. The majority of patients needed a dose escalation (10 patients) and of these, 6 patients were able to regain an ASAS40 response after the increased dose. In some patients, the dose was also reduced once again when a response was received with maintained disease control. Acknowledging the limited number of subjects, these results were considered sufficient to support a dose reduction to 100 mg in patients who achieved a response at week 16; and at increased risk of VTE, MACE and malignancy or patients aged 65 years of age and older. Treatment should be discontinued in patients who have shown no clinical response after 16 weeks of treatment. Further, in case of disease flare, the dose may be increased to 200 mg. For long term treatment, the lowest effective dose is recommended.

## 3.4. Unfavourable effects

During the placebo-controlled periods of studies TORTUGA (12 weeks) and OLINGUITO (16 weeks) in axSpA, the most common TEAE by PT was nasopharyngitis (22.32 E/100 PY in the placebo group and 16.35 E/100 PY in the filgotinib group). Drug-related TEAEs were more commonly reported in the filgotinib 200 mg group (12.1%) than in the placebo group (7.2%). This was primarily driven by a higher incidence of increased blood creatine phosphokinase (1.6%) and hypercholesterolaemia (1.6%), which are both adverse reactions of filgotinib and listed in the SmPC. In the overall data (filgotinib doses of 200 mg q.d. or LED from studies TORTUGA and OLINGUITO including both placebo-controlled and open-label treatment periods for up to 52 weeks), the most common TEAEs considered by the investigator to be related to the study drug were blood creatine phosphokinase increased, myositis, and hypercholesterolemia, all of which were reported only in the filgotinib 200 mg group. According to the MAH, all myositis events (7 cases) coincided with elevations in CK, and represent asymptomatic, or mildly symptomatic, CK elevations rather than clinically confirmed cases of myositis. CK elevations are reflected adequately in the SmPC. One death was reported in the studies of filgotinib in axSpA, corresponding to 0.2 E/100 PY, which is in line with the EAIRs for TEAEs leading to death reported in the studies of filgotinib in other indications (RA and UC). In the placebo-controlled period, the incidence rate of infections was similar in the filgotinib 200 mg and placebo groups (68.90 [95% CI: 52.32-89.07] and 81.83 [95% CI: 63.29-104.11] E/100 PY, respectively). Overall, in subjects treated with filgotinib, 7 subjects were reported with a serious infection (1.53 E/100 PY), 3 subjects with herpes zoster (0.66 E/100 PY, all reported as nonserious and Grade 1 or 2 in severity), and 1 subject with an opportunistic infection (Grade 2 ophthalmic herpes simplex, 0.22 E/100 PY). The use of filogitnib is contraindicated in patients with active tuberculosis or active serious infections and a warning on infections is already implemented in the product information. Serious and opportunistic infections and Herpes zoster are important identified risks in the RMP and will be further characterised in the axSpA population through the completion of the Study

Olinguito GLPG0634-CL-336 (Category 3 study in the RMP, final results expected in Q2 2027).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Regarding the important potential risks of MACE and VTE, there was a fatal MACE event in the filgotinib 200 mg group and one potential case of VTE reported in AxSpA subjects. Warning related to these risks are already implemented in the product information.

There were two cases of malignancies in the placebo-controlled pool, both in the filgotinib 200 mg group (EAIR: 2.12 E/100 PY). In the overall data, malignancies were reported in 4 subjects treated with filgotinib 200 mg (EAIR: 0.87 E/100 PY), which is in line with what is expected for the studied population. Malignancy and NMSC are important potential risks in the RMP; warnings related to these risks are already implemented in the product information.

There was a mean increase in liver transaminases (ALT and AST) observed during the first 8 weeks of filgotinib 200 mg treatment compared with placebo, the increase was small in magnitude and plateaued over time. There were no reports of clinically significant hepatic injury associated with the transaminase elevations. Considering the hepatotoxicity observed with other JAK inhibitors and the potential clinical relevance of the reported elevations, ALT increased and AST increased were added as adverse reactions (frequency: uncommon) in the product information.

JAK inhibitors are known to be teratogenic, and filgotinib is contraindicated during pregnancy.

## 3.5. Uncertainties and limitations about unfavourable effects

There were limited data in elderly subjects with axSpA since only 14 subjects (9 in the filgotinib 200 mg group and 5 in the placebo group) aged ≥65 years were included in the placebo-controlled parts of the studies. Thus, no meaningful conclusions could be made on the comparison between filgotinib and placebo in this age group. To complement the limited data in elderly with axSpA, the MAH discussed findings in elderly in the RA studies and concluded that a higher EAIRs of several AEs were observed in patients aged ≥65 years compared with patients aged &lt;65 years across both 200 mg and 100 mg dose groups, although this was consistent with the expected increase in background risk with age. There was no clear dose-dependent increase in serious or life-threatening AEs.

In order to minimise the safety risks known from the class of JAK inhibitors and data on filgotinib from other indications in patients 65 years of age and older and patients at increased risk for MACE, VTE and malignancy, the posology was adapted so that the higher dose of 200 mg is recommended in these patients at risk with axSpA for short term treatment (16 weeks) followed by a reduction to 100 mg in case of a response. Treatment should be discontinued in patients who have shown no clinical response after 16 weeks of treatment. In case of flare of the disease, the dose may be escalated to 200 mg once daily. For long term treatment, the lowest effective dose should be used.

## 3.7. Benefit-risk assessment and discussion

## 3.7.1. Importance of favourable and unfavourable effects

A clinically relevant and robust short-term effect on signs and symptoms of the disease as measured by ASAS40 has been demonstrated for filgotinib 200 mg in the target population of subjects with active axSpA, both r-axSpA and nr-axSpA. These results were supported by the secondary endpoints measuring different aspects of the disease such as disease activity, physical function, health related outcome and spinal mobility. It was although acknowledged that the results in the axSpA population were less prominent. Since the studied was population was not representative of a population primarily defined by intolerance to NSAIDs for nr-axSpA or conventional therapy for r-axSpA, the subgroups defined by intolerance were removed from the indication wording. Maintenance of efficacy of filgotinib was shown through 52 weeks for the majority of patients who had a response at Week 16. Efficacy was established in patients aged ≥65 years and in patients with pre-specified risk factors for MACE, VTE and malignancy that received a reduced dose.

The safety profile of filgotinib in the axSpA indication was considered largely consistent with the observed safety profile in the RA and UC populations.

Serious and opportunistic infections and Herpes zoster are important identified risks in the RMP and will be further characterised in the axSpA population through the completion of the Study OLINGUITO GLPG0634-CL-336 (Category 3 study in the RMP, final results expected in Q2 2027). The use of filogitnib is contraindicated in patients with active tuberculosis or active serious infections and a warning on infections is already included in the product information. MACE and VTE are important potential risks in the RMP and warning related to these risks are already implemented in the product information. Malignancy and NMSC are important potential risks in the RMP; warnings related to these risks are already implemented in the product information. Hyperlipidaemia is an important potential risk in the RMP; a warning on lipids is implemented in the product information.

Increases in liver transaminases were observed during the first weeks of treatment in patients with axSpA; ALT increased and AST increased were added as adverse reactions (frequency: uncommon) in the product information.

JAK inhibitors are known to be teratogenic, and filgotinib is contraindicated during pregnancy.

In order to minimise the safety risks known from the class of JAK inhibitors and data on filgotinib from other indications in patients 65 years of age and older and patients at increased risk for MACE, VTE and malignancy, the posology was adapted so that the higher dose of 200 mg is recommended in these patients at risk with axSpA for short term treatment (16 weeks) followed by a reduction to 100 mg in case of a response. Treatment should be discontinued in patients who have shown no clinical response after 16 weeks of treatment. In case of flare of the disease, the dose may be escalated to 200 mg once daily. For long term treatment, the lowest effective dose should be used.

## 3.7.2. Balance of benefits and risks

A clinically relevant and robust effect has been demonstrated for filgotinib 200 mg in the target population of subjects with active axSpA, both r-axSpA and nr-axSpA. The effect was shown on different aspects of the disease including signs and symptoms, disease activity, physical function, health related outcome and spinal mobility. Maintenance of the effect was shown for 52 weeks.

The safety profile of filgotinib in the axSpA population was overall consistent with the known safety profile of filgotinib in the RA and UC populations. In order to minimise the safety risks known from the class of JAK inhibitors and data on filgotinib from other indications in patients 65 years of age and older and patients at increased risk for MACE, VTE and malignancy, the posology was adapted to reduce the dose to 100 mg, after short treatment with the higher dose (200 mg).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Overall, the benefits of filgotinib outweigh the risks for the treatment of adult patients with active axSpA.

## 3.7.3. Additional considerations on the benefit-risk balance

Not applicable.

## 3.8. Conclusions

The overall benefit/risk balance of filgotinib is positive in the following indication:

Axial spondyloarthritis

Non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis (nr-axSpA)

Jyseleca is indicated for the treatment of active non-radiographic axial spondyloarthritis in adult patients with objective signs of inflammation as indicated by elevated C-reactive protein (CRP) and/or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), who have responded inadequately to nonsteroidal antiinflammatory drugs (NSAIDs).

Ankylosing spondylitis (AS, radiographic axial spondyloarthritis [r-axSpA])

Jyseleca is indicated for the treatment of active ankylosing spondylitis (radiographic axial spondyloarthritis) in adult patients who have responded inadequately to conventional therapy.

## 4. Recommendations

## Outcome

Based on the review of the submitted data, the CHMP considers the following variation acceptable and therefore recommends the variation to the terms of the Marketing Authorisation, concerning the following change:

| Variation accepted   | Variation accepted                                                                 | Type              |
|----------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.6.a                | C.6.a) Addition of a new therapeutic indication or modification of an approved one | Variation type II |

Extension of indication to include treatment of axial spondyloarthritis in adult patients with active nonradiographic axial spondyloarthritis with objective signs of inflammation as indicated by elevated Creactive protein (CRP) and/or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), who have responded inadequately to nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs (NSAIDs) and in adult patients with active ankylosing spondylitis (radiographic axial spondyloarthritis) who have responded inadequately to conventional therapy for Jyseleca, based on interim results from Study GLPG0634-CL-336 (OLINGUITO); this is a Phase 3 randomised, placebo-controlled, double-blind, parallel-group program to evaluate efficacy and safety of filgotinib in adult subjects with active axial spondyloarthritis. As a consequence, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.8, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 and 6.6 of the SmPC are updated. The Package Leaflet is updated in accordance. Editorial changes have been introduced.

Version 8.0 of the RMP is agreed.

The variation leads to amendments to the annex(es) I, II, and IIIB and to the Risk Management Plan (RMP).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>
